## Phụ lục — Rà soát chương trình Thuận Nhiên

Nguồn: `docs/curriculum/nha-thuan-nhien-audit-approved.md`  
SHA-256: `b557a73bd80529f4773866bf6abbc830206d3e661a4d0d6e8f403acf69d5b6f2`

# Nhà Thuận Nhiên — Approved Curriculum Audit Decisions

**Status:** Round-1 audit recommendations approved on 2026-09-21.

## Implementation policy

- **STRENGTH** — preserve; do not simplify or delete when normalizing the curriculum.
- **ERROR** — correct the error, while preserving traceability to the original source.
- **REVIEW** — implement the recommended revision/clarification, not a broad AI rewrite.
- **VARIATION** — preserve as a legitimate Nhà Thuận Nhiên variation; clarify rationale/progression where recommended.
- Never fill undocumented blanks with invented curriculum content.
- Keep source workbook intact as historical/source material.

## Approved audit items

### ALL — Program-wide — STRENGTH (High)
- **Location:** Across workbook
- **Current / observation:** Nhà Thuận Nhiên contains much richer fields than a simple topic list: timing, approach, activities, developmental intention, skills, notes, arts, practical work and local culture.
- **Why flagged:** This richness is an asset and should not be compressed to the schema depth of the other reference curricula.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve all meaningful fields in the source layer; design the website schema to allow optional rich fields rather than deleting them.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf Resources says its vertical curriculum is a framework/impulse to be adapted to the cultural environment.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – Vertical Curriculum
- **Decision:** Keep as-is

### ALL — Program-wide — STRENGTH (High)
- **Location:** Across workbook
- **Current / observation:** Strong integration of storytelling, arts, music, handwork, gardening, cooking, building, field trips and local cultural material.
- **Why flagged:** These are broadly consistent with Waldorf's experiential, artistic and practical orientation.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve as a defining identity of Nhà Thuận Nhiên.
- **Reference/rationale:** AWSNA identifies artistic approach, experience-to-concept, rhythm/repetition and observation as core components.
- **Source:** AWSNA Principles
- **Decision:** Keep as-is

### ALL — Program-wide — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** Across workbook
- **Current / observation:** Operational school-year details (2024 dates, teacher names, training absences, camps, festival dates) are mixed with reusable curriculum content.
- **Why flagged:** Historical operational data is useful but can become misleading if shown as timeless curriculum.
- **Approved handling:** Keep raw source unchanged; in normalized curriculum separate reusable pedagogy from 'historical schedule / implementation notes'.
- **Reference/rationale:** Curriculum should remain evolvable while preserving traceability.
- **Source:** Workbook structure
- **Decision:** Revise

### ALL — Program-wide — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** Across workbook
- **Current / observation:** Anthroposophical/Waldorf interpretive language, observable developmental milestones, and scientific/medical claims are often written in the same voice.
- **Why flagged:** Readers may not know which statements are pedagogical images, traditional Waldorf concepts, teacher observations, or empirically established science.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve the language but classify rationale where useful: 'Waldorf/anthroposophical lens', 'teacher observation', 'developmental reference', 'scientific claim'. Verify scientific claims separately.
- **Reference/rationale:** AWSNA explicitly grounds Waldorf in anthroposophical child development; this does not make every developmental formulation an empirical scientific claim.
- **Source:** AWSNA Principles
- **Decision:** Revise

### ALL — Program-wide — REVIEW (Medium)
- **Location:** Across workbook
- **Current / observation:** Some rows are very detailed while others contain only a block title or blank outcomes.
- **Why flagged:** Automatically filling every blank with AI would create synthetic curriculum and erase the distinction between teacher-authored and generated material.
- **Approved handling:** Do not auto-fill blanks. Mark them 'not yet documented' and invite teacher review.
- **Reference/rationale:** Faculty review and freedom in teaching are part of Waldorf program development.
- **Source:** AWSNA Principles
- **Decision:** Revise

### ALL — Program-wide — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** Public website layer
- **Current / observation:** Teacher names and personal operational notes are embedded in the workbook.
- **Why flagged:** They may be useful internally but are not necessarily intended for public publication.
- **Approved handling:** Retain in source/archive; decide explicitly which personnel/operational metadata is public before exposing it on saokhue.xuvi.vn.
- **Reference/rationale:** Privacy / publication governance issue, not a Waldorf-content correction.
- **Source:** Workbook structure
- **Decision:** Revise

### MN 2-3 — Data quality — ERROR (High)
- **Location:** D6 / date field
- **Current / observation:** Raw Excel value 45571 appears in the time column between the June and August entries.
- **Why flagged:** It is a spreadsheet date serial / formatting anomaly and is not usable as curriculum text. Direct conversion would yield 2024-10-06, which conflicts with its position and the nearby Đoan Ngọ note.
- **Approved handling:** Do not guess. Preserve raw value in source metadata and mark the intended date for teacher confirmation.
- **Reference/rationale:** High-confidence spreadsheet anomaly.
- **Source:** Workbook
- **Decision:** Correct

### MN 2-3 — Development — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** G2 / language milestone
- **Current / observation:** The 2–3 age-band says children 'use one word to express a whole sentence'.
- **Why flagged:** This may describe some younger two-year-olds, but it is too low/too broad as a general 2–3-year developmental expectation. CDC's 30-month milestone includes about 50 words and two or more words together with an action word.
- **Approved handling:** Replace a single age-band outcome with age-banded observational guidance; retain the original as historical teacher wording.
- **Reference/rationale:** Developmental milestones should be observational guides, not uniform pass/fail outcomes.
- **Source:** CDC 30-month milestones
- **Decision:** Revise

### MN 2-3 — Pedagogy — STRENGTH (High)
- **Location:** Whole sheet
- **Current / observation:** Seasonal circle activities, stories, baking, cleaning and simple nature-linked rhythms dominate the plan.
- **Why flagged:** This is highly compatible with Waldorf early-childhood emphasis on rhythm, imagination, creative play and practical activity.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve.
- **Reference/rationale:** AWSNA early childhood highlights storytelling, music, creative play, nature, daily rhythm and observation.
- **Source:** AWSNA Early Childhood
- **Decision:** Keep as-is

### MN 3-5 — Development — REVIEW (Medium)
- **Location:** H2 / developmental goals
- **Current / observation:** A single list covers physical, emotional and self-care expectations across ages 3 to 5.
- **Why flagged:** The developmental span is large; wording can be misread as all children in the group being expected to reach the same milestones.
- **Approved handling:** Keep the list but reframe as observations across the age band, with individual variation and age-specific notes where important.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf assessment is observation-based; modern developmental guidance also treats milestones by age and variation.
- **Source:** AWSNA Early Childhood; CDC
- **Decision:** Revise

### MN 3-5 — Pedagogy — STRENGTH (High)
- **Location:** Whole sheet
- **Current / observation:** Imaginative play, seasonal circles, stories, imitation, self-care through rhythm and social play are central.
- **Why flagged:** Strong fit with Waldorf early childhood.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve.
- **Reference/rationale:** Rhythm, warmth, imitation and play are central in Waldorf early childhood.
- **Source:** AWSNA
- **Decision:** Keep as-is

### MN nhỏ 3-5 — Scientific wording — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** J11/K11 / bread
- **Current / observation:** The bread activity says wheat 'absorbs solar energy' and making bread 'brings/nourishes warmth'.
- **Why flagged:** As poetic/pedagogical imagery this can be meaningful; as a literal physiological/scientific claim it is too strong and ambiguous.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve the image if teachers value it, but label it as pedagogical/poetic rationale rather than scientific explanation.
- **Reference/rationale:** Distinguish experiential image from scientific claim.
- **Source:** Workbook + science literacy principle
- **Decision:** Revise

### MN nhỏ 3-5 — Assessment — REVIEW (Medium)
- **Location:** K12–K13 / storytelling
- **Current / observation:** Story outcomes emphasize sitting quietly, sitting straight and remaining still.
- **Why flagged:** Some listening structure is reasonable, but compliance/posture should not become the primary developmental measure for young children.
- **Approved handling:** Reframe primary aims around listening, language, imagination, relationship and participation; observe regulation without rigid posture expectations.
- **Reference/rationale:** AWSNA early childhood emphasizes imagination, play, language/social development and observation.
- **Source:** AWSNA Early Childhood
- **Decision:** Revise

### MN nhỏ 3-5 — Safeguarding — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** H15/I15 / hand games
- **Current / observation:** Teacher touches children's hands/body in soothing finger games.
- **Why flagged:** Warm touch can be appropriate, but public curriculum should state child assent, bodily autonomy and safeguarding boundaries.
- **Approved handling:** Keep the activity; add an explicit 'child can decline / ask first / avoid private-body areas / follow safeguarding policy' note.
- **Reference/rationale:** Modern safeguarding requirement; not a rejection of Waldorf touch/rhythm practices.
- **Source:** Safeguarding best practice
- **Decision:** Revise

### MN nhỏ 3-5 — Pedagogy — STRENGTH (High)
- **Location:** Rows 3–16
- **Current / observation:** The sheet documents rhythm in unusually useful detail: outdoor play, circle, indoor free play, art, baking, story, cleaning, transitions and songs.
- **Why flagged:** This is more useful than a simple topic list and should not be lost.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve the full daily/weekly rhythm fields.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf early childhood uses rhythm and imitation as core methods.
- **Source:** AWSNA Early Childhood
- **Decision:** Keep as-is

### MN lớn 5-7 — Development — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** D3 / 'crisis around 5.5'
- **Current / observation:** A 'crisis around 5.5 years' is described as if it were a predictable developmental event.
- **Why flagged:** This may be a Waldorf/anthroposophical observational motif, but should not be presented as a universal clinical milestone.
- **Approved handling:** Keep it as a Waldorf/teacher-observation lens with explicit individual variation; do not diagnose children from it.
- **Reference/rationale:** AWSNA recognizes archetypal developmental phases while emphasizing each child's unique expression.
- **Source:** AWSNA Principles
- **Decision:** Revise

### MN lớn 5-7 — Scientific wording — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** I7/J7 / beeswax
- **Current / observation:** Beeswax is described as containing accumulated sunlight warmth that spreads warmth through the child's body.
- **Why flagged:** Meaningful as imaginative language, but not as a literal scientific/medical mechanism.
- **Approved handling:** Keep as poetic image or sensory description; remove/qualify causal health claims.
- **Reference/rationale:** Separate artistic image from scientific claim.
- **Source:** Workbook
- **Decision:** Revise

### MN lớn 5-7 — Safeguarding — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** Rows 13 / touch on face/body
- **Current / observation:** Finger games include stroking the child's hand, face or body.
- **Why flagged:** Needs explicit consent/assent and safeguarding boundaries.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve activity with child-choice and safe-touch protocol.
- **Reference/rationale:** Safeguarding issue.
- **Source:** Workbook
- **Decision:** Revise

### MN lớn 5-7 — Pedagogy — REVIEW (Medium)
- **Location:** Row 14 / pentatonic music
- **Current / observation:** Pentatonic music is said to 'nourish dreamlike consciousness'.
- **Why flagged:** This is a traditional Waldorf pedagogical rationale, not an empirically established developmental effect.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve if useful, but classify as Waldorf pedagogical rationale rather than science.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf-specific interpretive claim.
- **Source:** Workbook
- **Decision:** Revise

### MN lớn 5-7 — Pedagogy — STRENGTH (High)
- **Location:** Rows 3–14
- **Current / observation:** Rich free play, nature, watercolor, beeswax, handwork, bread, storytelling and household responsibility.
- **Why flagged:** Strong Waldorf early-childhood coherence and unusually detailed implementation notes.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve.
- **Reference/rationale:** AWSNA Early Childhood.
- **Source:** AWSNA
- **Decision:** Keep as-is

### MN 5-7 — Data quality — ERROR (High)
- **Location:** B2 / age
- **Current / observation:** Age cell contains raw Excel value 45419.
- **Why flagged:** Spreadsheet date/number formatting error; it must not appear as the child's age.
- **Approved handling:** Use sheet identity 'MN 5-7' for navigation; retain raw value only in source audit; ask teacher to confirm intended age text.
- **Reference/rationale:** High-confidence data anomaly.
- **Source:** Workbook
- **Decision:** Correct

### MN 5-7 — School readiness — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** F2 / physical signs
- **Current / observation:** The list includes touching the opposite ear over the head, tooth change and body proportions as school-readiness indicators.
- **Why flagged:** These signs occur in Waldorf readiness literature, but modern school readiness is multi-domain; no single physical sign should determine readiness.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve as one observational tradition, explicitly not a pass/fail criterion; combine with language, motor, social-emotional, attention and learning observations.
- **Reference/rationale:** Modern school-readiness sources define readiness across multiple domains.
- **Source:** PubMed/HRSA school readiness; Waldorf readiness tradition
- **Decision:** Revise

### MN 5-7 — Development — REVIEW (Medium)
- **Location:** F2 / social-emotional
- **Current / observation:** The list includes attention to gender and 'feelings toward the opposite sex' as a developmental expectation.
- **Why flagged:** This is not necessary as a universal school-readiness/developmental criterion and can become normatively interpreted.
- **Approved handling:** Remove from core milestone checklist or reframe broadly as growing awareness of relationships/identity without expectation.
- **Reference/rationale:** Avoid treating optional social patterns as universal developmental outcomes.
- **Source:** Workbook
- **Decision:** Revise

### MN 5-7 — Localization — STRENGTH (High)
- **Location:** Rows 2–11
- **Current / observation:** Curriculum follows Hội An/Central Vietnam seasons and community life: harvest, beach/fishing, Đoan Ngọ, Mid-Autumn, rain/floods, local foods, Tết.
- **Why flagged:** This is a strong example of adapting Waldorf rhythm to local climate and culture rather than importing a European calendar.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve prominently.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf Resources explicitly says curriculum ideas must be adapted to the cultural environment.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – Vertical Curriculum
- **Decision:** Keep as-is

### Lớp 1 — Data quality — ERROR (High)
- **Location:** E11
- **Current / observation:** Date is written '7/102024 > 25/10/2024'.
- **Why flagged:** Malformed date.
- **Approved handling:** Normalize only after confirming intended '07/10/2024'. Keep original in audit.
- **Reference/rationale:** High-confidence typographic error.
- **Source:** Workbook
- **Decision:** Correct

### Lớp 1 — Data quality — ERROR (High)
- **Location:** E14
- **Current / observation:** Date range is '9/12/2024 > 3/1/2024'.
- **Why flagged:** End date precedes start date; likely the year should be 2025.
- **Approved handling:** Flag for confirmation; likely normalize to 03/01/2025 after teacher approval.
- **Reference/rationale:** High-confidence chronology error.
- **Source:** Workbook
- **Decision:** Correct

### Lớp 1 — Pedagogy — STRENGTH (High)
- **Location:** Whole sheet
- **Current / observation:** Letters and numbers are introduced through story/image/movement; form drawing, nature, handwork, baking, watercolor, music and circle work are retained.
- **Why flagged:** Strong match to Waldorf grades approach and much richer than a topic-only curriculum.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve detail.
- **Reference/rationale:** AWSNA grades: academic subjects integrated with visual, performing and practical arts.
- **Source:** AWSNA Grades
- **Decision:** Keep as-is

### Lớp 2 — Data quality — ERROR (High)
- **Location:** E37
- **Current / observation:** Date '1/11-31/11' uses November 31.
- **Why flagged:** Invalid calendar date.
- **Approved handling:** Confirm intended end date (likely 30/11) before normalization.
- **Reference/rationale:** High-confidence calendar error.
- **Source:** Workbook
- **Decision:** Correct

### Lớp 2 — Pedagogy — STRENGTH (High)
- **Location:** Rows 3–27
- **Current / observation:** Fables/saint stories, rhythmic multiplication, nature experience, form drawing, music, handwork, bread, clay and watercolor are integrated.
- **Why flagged:** Coherent Waldorf-style balance of image, rhythm, academics and practical arts.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve.
- **Reference/rationale:** AWSNA core principles: artistic approach, experience-to-concept, rhythm and repetition.
- **Source:** AWSNA Principles
- **Decision:** Keep as-is

### Lớp 3 — Data quality — ERROR (High)
- **Location:** E44
- **Current / observation:** Date '1/11-31/11' uses November 31.
- **Why flagged:** Invalid calendar date.
- **Approved handling:** Confirm intended end date before normalization.
- **Reference/rationale:** High-confidence calendar error.
- **Source:** Workbook
- **Decision:** Correct

### Lớp 3 — Pedagogy — STRENGTH (High)
- **Location:** Rows 3–31
- **Current / observation:** Measurement, building, money/exchange, gardening, cooking, handwork and music are connected to narrative blocks.
- **Why flagged:** Very strong practical-life coherence for Grade 3 and a distinctive strength of this curriculum.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve all practical implementation details.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf curriculum commonly emphasizes practical real-world work in this developmental period; experience-to-concept is core.
- **Source:** AWSNA Principles
- **Decision:** Keep as-is

### Lớp 3 — Localization — VARIATION (High)
- **Location:** G3, G6, G8
- **Current / observation:** Biblical/creation and 'Journey to the Promised Land' material sits alongside 'Văn Lang dị sử'.
- **Why flagged:** This is a deliberate-looking Vietnamese adaptation rather than a simple classical sequence.
- **Approved handling:** Do not 'correct' it automatically. Clarify source/story status and pedagogical rationale; preserve if teachers affirm the sequence.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf Resources encourages cultural adaptation; curriculum need not be a European copy.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – Vertical Curriculum
- **Decision:** Keep as-is

### Lớp 3 — Terminology — REVIEW (Medium)
- **Location:** J12/J13/J17/J18
- **Current / observation:** Repeated rationale says children 'begin to come down to earth'.
- **Why flagged:** This is anthroposophical pedagogical language, not a measurable developmental fact.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve under a clearly labeled Waldorf/anthroposophical rationale; pair with observable educational aims.
- **Reference/rationale:** Distinguish interpretive framework from observable outcomes.
- **Source:** Workbook
- **Decision:** Revise

### Lớp 4 — Pedagogy — STRENGTH (High)
- **Location:** Rows 2–12
- **Current / observation:** Norse mythology, history of writing, local geography, human/animal study, fractions, two-part singing, đàn nhị, embroidery and clay form a coherent artistic/practical year.
- **Why flagged:** Rich and broadly recognizable Waldorf Grade 4 structure with meaningful Vietnamese localization.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve.
- **Reference/rationale:** AWSNA grades + Waldorf Resources cultural adaptation.
- **Source:** AWSNA; Waldorf Resources
- **Decision:** Keep as-is

### Lớp 4 — Cultural language — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** G16 / English
- **Current / observation:** English unit uses the broad label 'Thổ dân Da đỏ'.
- **Why flagged:** The term is outdated/overgeneralizing and can flatten many distinct Indigenous nations and cultures.
- **Approved handling:** Update terminology and identify specific peoples/nations and sources; review stories/images for stereotypes. Preserve the learning intention rather than the label.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf history guidance rejects ranking/discriminating cultures and emphasizes integrity of each culture.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – History
- **Decision:** Revise

### Lớp 4 — Religious inclusion — REVIEW (Medium)
- **Location:** I18 / English verse
- **Current / observation:** Verse includes 'God sleeps in the minerals'.
- **Why flagged:** A spiritual verse can belong to a school culture, but public curriculum should distinguish devotional practice from language/art material and consider family diversity.
- **Approved handling:** Retain if intentional; label context and ensure it is consistent with the school's inclusive/non-sectarian policy.
- **Reference/rationale:** AWSNA describes Waldorf schools as non-sectarian while recognizing a spiritual dimension.
- **Source:** AWSNA FAQ
- **Decision:** Revise

### Lớp 5 — Data structure — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** Rows 10–17 vs 32–39
- **Current / observation:** The sheet contains two different/overlapping schedules for the Aug–Jan period (e.g., one sequence includes Egypt–Greece, Tộc Việt, freehand geometry; another lists Tây Nguyên, combined Persia–Mesopotamia–Egypt, Greece/Alexander, etc.).
- **Why flagged:** This may be revision history or two versions, not a single coherent final schedule.
- **Approved handling:** Do not merge automatically. Preserve both as source variants and ask teachers which sequence is current; then record the decision.
- **Reference/rationale:** Source-version conflict.
- **Source:** Workbook
- **Decision:** Revise

### Lớp 5 — Localization — STRENGTH (High)
- **Location:** G15, G32 and history blocks
- **Current / observation:** Ancient-culture sequence is supplemented with Tộc Việt and Tây Nguyên epic/culture.
- **Why flagged:** Strong local adaptation with potential to root history in Vietnamese cultural memory.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve; improve source attribution and historical/mythic distinctions where needed.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf Resources asks curricula to be adapted to cultural environment and history teaching to honor cultural diversity.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – History
- **Decision:** Keep as-is

### Lớp 5 — Historical framing — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** J8 and ancient-culture rationales
- **Current / observation:** Language such as humanity gradually 'incarnating/coming down to earth' is used to characterize ancient cultures.
- **Why flagged:** This is an anthroposophical developmental interpretation, not established historical fact; if presented literally it may distort history or imply cultural hierarchy.
- **Approved handling:** Keep as a labeled pedagogical lens if the faculty wants it, while teaching myths, archaeology and documented history distinctly and avoiding value hierarchies between cultures.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf history guidance emphasizes factual events, documented evidence, multiple perspectives and rejects ranking cultures.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – History
- **Decision:** Revise

### Lớp 5 — Cultural language — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** G20–G21 / English
- **Current / observation:** English unit uses 'Thổ dân da đỏ'.
- **Why flagged:** Outdated/overbroad terminology.
- **Approved handling:** Use specific Indigenous peoples/nations and vetted contemporary sources.
- **Reference/rationale:** Respect cultural integrity and diversity.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – History
- **Decision:** Revise

### Lớp 6 — Data quality — ERROR (Medium)
- **Location:** G5/G8
- **Current / observation:** Rows use 'Nghĩ' / 'Nghĩ hè'.
- **Why flagged:** Likely typo for 'Nghỉ' / 'Nghỉ hè'.
- **Approved handling:** Correct after quick source confirmation.
- **Reference/rationale:** Likely typographic error.
- **Source:** Workbook
- **Decision:** Correct

### Lớp 6 — Science framing — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** H4/I4 / astronomy
- **Current / observation:** Astronomy is framed partly as the influence/relationship of Sun, Moon and stars with human life.
- **Why flagged:** Observable effects (day/night, seasons, tides, navigation, calendars) are scientific; astrological or spiritual effects should not be presented as established astronomy without clear distinction.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve cultural/poetic stories, but separate observational astronomy from symbolic/astrological interpretations.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf science emphasizes observation and phenomenological inquiry.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – Physics/Natural Science
- **Decision:** Revise

### Lớp 6 — Safety — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** G13, G19 / bow, spear, knife forging
- **Current / observation:** Students make bows/arrows/spears and forge a small knife.
- **Why flagged:** Practical craft can be educational, but the activity requires explicit tool safety, supervision, storage/transport rules and age-appropriate risk assessment.
- **Approved handling:** Do not delete automatically; add a safety protocol and teacher qualification requirements before public implementation.
- **Reference/rationale:** Risk-management issue, not inherently a Waldorf conflict.
- **Source:** Workbook
- **Decision:** Revise

### Lớp 6 — Pedagogy — STRENGTH (High)
- **Location:** G3–G19
- **Current / observation:** Phenomenological physics, astronomy observation, Roman history, percentages/economics, mineralogy, Vietnam geography, compass geometry, forging and handwork.
- **Why flagged:** Strong integration of observation, exactness, real-world math and craft.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf Resources physics starts Grade 6 with phenomena/experiment; math and crafts develop exactness.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – Physics
- **Decision:** Keep as-is

### Lớp 7 — Data quality — ERROR (Medium)
- **Location:** G5
- **Current / observation:** Row is labeled 'Nghĩ'.
- **Why flagged:** Likely typo for 'Nghỉ'.
- **Approved handling:** Correct after source confirmation.
- **Reference/rationale:** Likely typographic error.
- **Source:** Workbook
- **Decision:** Correct

### Lớp 7 — Health/science — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** G6–I6 / yin-yang, martial arts and diet
- **Current / observation:** Traditional yin-yang concepts are used to teach martial arts and food choices.
- **Why flagged:** This can be valuable cultural material, but traditional frameworks should be distinguished from evidence-based nutrition/medical claims.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve as cultural/philosophical practice; add accurate physiology/nutrition where health claims are made and avoid presenting one dietary system as medically universal.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf curriculum can adapt culturally; science lessons still require careful observation and factual accuracy.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – Vertical Curriculum/Natural Science
- **Decision:** Revise

### Lớp 7 — Health/relationships — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** G11/J11 / puberty and sex education
- **Current / observation:** The block mentions sex education, self-respect and care for the body.
- **Why flagged:** Good topic, but the curriculum detail should explicitly cover accurate anatomy, puberty, consent/boundaries, respectful relationships and where appropriate reproductive health.
- **Approved handling:** Keep the block and strengthen the documented learning aims so they are not only moral/affective.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf Resources says puberty, reproduction and sexuality need space in biology lessons.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – Natural Science
- **Decision:** Revise

### Lớp 7 — Localization — STRENGTH (High)
- **Location:** G7–G13
- **Current / observation:** Vietnam geography, Lân/Sư/Cẩu, Tuồng and local arts sit beside Renaissance history, chemistry and algebra.
- **Why flagged:** Distinctive local cultural integration.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve.
- **Reference/rationale:** Cultural adaptation is explicitly encouraged.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – Vertical Curriculum
- **Decision:** Keep as-is

### Lớp 8 — Pedagogy — STRENGTH (High)
- **Location:** G3–G12
- **Current / observation:** Revolution/industrial history, biography, algebra/geometry, human anatomy, Asia geography, local-culture fieldwork and increasing independent study.
- **Why flagged:** Strong bridge toward adolescent independent judgment while retaining artistic and experiential work.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf history Grade 8 reaches modern social/economic history; adolescence shifts toward independent thinking.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – History; AWSNA
- **Decision:** Keep as-is

### Lớp 8 — Terminology/science — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** G10 / 'Sinh học - Khoa học tính khí con người'
- **Current / observation:** Temperament is labeled 'science'.
- **Why flagged:** If this refers to the traditional Waldorf four-temperament model, it is a pedagogical/anthroposophical lens rather than established biological science.
- **Approved handling:** Rename or annotate so students/parents can distinguish a Waldorf interpretive framework from empirical biology.
- **Reference/rationale:** Keep interpretive frameworks transparent.
- **Source:** Workbook
- **Decision:** Revise

### Lớp 8 — Modernization — REVIEW (Medium)
- **Location:** K7 / 'bước đầu sử dụng công nghệ'
- **Current / observation:** Technology appears only briefly as a learning tool.
- **Why flagged:** This is an opportunity to define age-appropriate digital literacy rather than either ignoring technology or using it uncritically.
- **Approved handling:** Add a small documented progression: typing/research/source evaluation/privacy/digital judgment, while preserving Waldorf's developmentally cautious approach.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf Resources recommends typing in Grades 6–8 and regular ICT in high school.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – ICT
- **Decision:** Revise

### Lớp 9 — Religious/spiritual practice — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** L3 and J7
- **Current / observation:** Students are directed to spend at least one hour/day in meditation or stillness; other outcomes include 'breaking ego-attachment'.
- **Why flagged:** This goes beyond teaching about spirituality and becomes a prescribed spiritual practice/outcome. That may be intentional school identity, but it is not a universal requirement of Waldorf education.
- **Approved handling:** Do not delete silently. Ask faculty whether this is an explicit Nhà Thuận Nhiên commitment. If retained, distinguish optional contemplative practice, school culture and academic curriculum; consider inclusion and student autonomy.
- **Reference/rationale:** AWSNA describes Waldorf schools as non-sectarian and not espousing a particular religious doctrine.
- **Source:** AWSNA FAQ
- **Decision:** Revise

### Lớp 9 — Health/science — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** G7–J7 / dưỡng sinh, yin-yang food chemistry
- **Current / observation:** Macrobiotic/dưỡng sinh, yin-yang and food chemistry are combined with claims about body/mind and natural laws.
- **Why flagged:** Fermentation, food chemistry and cooking are excellent; broader health claims need evidence and clear boundaries between tradition and science.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve practical food chemistry; label traditional-health frameworks and verify health claims separately.
- **Reference/rationale:** Science/health accuracy issue.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – Natural Science
- **Decision:** Revise

### Lớp 9 — Human development — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** G14–K14 / four-element temperament
- **Current / observation:** Students learn Earth-Water-Fire-Air temperament and a skill goal says they can identify other people's personalities.
- **Why flagged:** There is risk of turning a reflective metaphor into labeling/typing classmates.
- **Approved handling:** If retained, use it for self-reflection and perspective-taking, not diagnosis or fixed labels; explicitly state limits.
- **Reference/rationale:** Respect individuality; each child's development is unique.
- **Source:** AWSNA Principles
- **Decision:** Revise

### Lớp 9 — Pedagogy — STRENGTH (High)
- **Location:** Whole sheet
- **Current / observation:** Modern revolutions/world history, algebra/economics, food transformation, Cham culture/music, woodworking, technology physics, photography, marine field ecology, sewing, jewelry and art/music.
- **Why flagged:** Unusually broad practical/cultural curriculum with strong real-world engagement.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf adolescence supports meaningful engagement with the world and judgment through experience.
- **Source:** AWSNA; Waldorf Resources
- **Decision:** Keep as-is

### Lớp 10 — Modernization — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** G13 / computer technology
- **Current / observation:** Block focuses on computer hardware, how computers work, and responsibility; skill target is disassembling/repair.
- **Why flagged:** Good foundation, but a current high-school ICT sequence should also address logic, networks/software, data/privacy and informed judgment; AI is now an important topic.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve hardware/repair and expand rather than replace it with digital systems, source evaluation, privacy/security, algorithms and age-appropriate AI literacy.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf Resources ICT Grade 10 emphasizes computer structure/function, networks and critical use; Grades 11–12 address algorithms, AI and social implications.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – ICT
- **Decision:** Revise

### Lớp 10 — Pedagogy — STRENGTH (High)
- **Location:** G3–G17
- **Current / observation:** History, economic math/trigonometry, physics, anatomy, chemistry, art/music history, climate/ecology, computing, printmaking and ensemble music.
- **Why flagged:** Strong interdisciplinary and contemporary breadth.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf high school supports rigorous academics plus art/practical work.
- **Source:** AWSNA; Waldorf Resources
- **Decision:** Keep as-is

### Lớp 10 — Science framing — REVIEW (Medium)
- **Location:** I8/J8 / brain and moral conscience
- **Current / observation:** The biology block links brain systems with perception, thought, memory and 'moral conscience'.
- **Why flagged:** The relationship between brain function and moral judgment is complex; wording can read as a simple biological localization claim.
- **Approved handling:** Keep neuroscience content but use more cautious language and distinguish biological correlates from philosophical/moral questions.
- **Reference/rationale:** Avoid over-reduction of human capacities to a single organ/mechanism.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – Natural Science
- **Decision:** Revise

### Lớp 11 — Science accuracy — ERROR (High)
- **Location:** J7 / cell biology
- **Current / observation:** Goal says microscope work lets students 'observe molecules'.
- **Why flagged:** Standard light microscopy resolves roughly hundreds of nanometers; ordinary classroom microscopy can observe cells and many cellular structures, not individual molecules.
- **Approved handling:** Change to 'observe cells, microorganisms and selected cellular structures/specimens' unless specialized super-resolution methods are actually used.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf Grade 11 biology explicitly recommends regular microscope observations of organisms/specimens; optical microscopy has diffraction-limited resolution.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – Natural Science; Nikon MicroscopyU
- **Decision:** Correct

### Lớp 11 — History coherence — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** G3–I3
- **Current / observation:** Block is titled modern history 'from Renaissance to Enlightenment', but central content is Trần Nhân Tông (1258–1308).
- **Why flagged:** The chronology/title/content do not match. Trần Nhân Tông may be a valuable Vietnamese focus, but the framing needs clarification.
- **Approved handling:** Ask whether the block is comparative history of religion/politics, Vietnamese medieval history, or Renaissance–Enlightenment. Retitle/resequence rather than deleting Trần Nhân Tông.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf history sequence is a framework, but historical chronology and source clarity still matter.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – History
- **Decision:** Revise

### Lớp 11 — Religious/spiritual practice — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** L3, L4, J12/L12
- **Current / observation:** Daily meditation and 'breaking ego-attachment' are explicit student practices/outcomes.
- **Why flagged:** These reflect a particular spiritual path more than a neutral academic exploration.
- **Approved handling:** Clarify whether this is optional contemplative practice, Nhà Thuận Nhiên ethos, or required curriculum; ensure student autonomy and pluralism.
- **Reference/rationale:** AWSNA describes Waldorf schools as non-sectarian, recognizing diverse religions without espousing one doctrine.
- **Source:** AWSNA FAQ
- **Decision:** Revise

### Lớp 11 — Pedagogy — STRENGTH (High)
- **Location:** G6–G15
- **Current / observation:** Projective geometry/infinity, cell microscopy, element chemistry, electromagnetism/radiation, geography, astronomy, technology, sculpture and music theater.
- **Why flagged:** Much of this is strikingly close to Waldorf high-school developmental sequencing and is academically rich.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf Resources places projective geometry, cell/microscopic biology, elements and EM/radiation in this upper-school phase.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – Math/Natural Science/Chemistry/Physics
- **Decision:** Keep as-is

### Lớp 11 — Modernization — REVIEW (Medium)
- **Location:** No explicit ICT progression
- **Current / observation:** After Grade 10 hardware, no clear Grade 11 computing/software block is documented.
- **Why flagged:** If the workbook is intended as a complete curriculum, this leaves a modern gap.
- **Approved handling:** Consider adding programming/algorithms, data/privacy, AI and human-machine judgment, without reducing existing arts/science.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf Resources Grade 11 ICT explicitly includes programming, algorithms, data security and AI/human-machine questions.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – ICT
- **Decision:** Revise

### Lớp 12 — Data quality — ERROR (High)
- **Location:** B3
- **Current / observation:** Sheet is 'Lớp 12' but class cell contains 10.
- **Why flagged:** Clear structural inconsistency.
- **Approved handling:** Use sheet identity as provisional Grade 12 for navigation; preserve the raw value and confirm before correcting source-derived data.
- **Reference/rationale:** High-confidence source anomaly.
- **Source:** Workbook
- **Decision:** Correct

### Lớp 12 — Religion/history — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** G3–L3 and L10
- **Current / observation:** The program says comparative philosophies/world religions but emphasizes the Buddha; another row's note is simply 'PHẬT GIÁO'.
- **Why flagged:** This may be a deliberate Nhà Thuận Nhiên identity, but the curriculum should clarify whether it is comparative religious studies or formation within a specific Buddhist orientation.
- **Approved handling:** Do not remove Buddhism. Clarify learning goal, include multiple traditions if claiming comparison, and distinguish academic study from prescribed spiritual adherence.
- **Reference/rationale:** AWSNA: Waldorf schools are non-sectarian and seek understanding of world cultures/religions.
- **Source:** AWSNA FAQ
- **Decision:** Revise

### Lớp 12 — Health/science — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** K9 / Đông Y
- **Current / observation:** Skill goal includes 'self-healing'.
- **Why flagged:** The phrase is medically broad and may imply students can treat conditions beyond safe self-care.
- **Approved handling:** Keep traditional medicine literacy and practical self-care, but change to safe self-care, health literacy, limits of home care, and when professional medical care is needed.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf biology resources explicitly discuss clarifying the limits of lay medicine and when professional help is necessary.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – Natural Science
- **Decision:** Revise

### Lớp 12 — Pedagogy — STRENGTH (High)
- **Location:** G12–G15
- **Current / observation:** Architecture, ensemble/composition and a six-month self-chosen graduation thesis/project.
- **Why flagged:** Excellent capstone structure for independent judgment, synthesis and purposeful work.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve prominently.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf Resources identifies architecture as a Grade 12 art theme; upper-school pedagogy supports independent judgment.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – Art Appreciation
- **Decision:** Keep as-is

### Lớp 12 — Science sequence — VARIATION (Medium)
- **Location:** G6 / atomic physics
- **Current / observation:** Atomic physics is placed in Grade 12.
- **Why flagged:** A common Waldorf Resources sequence introduces atomic/radiation physics in Grade 11 and uses Grade 12 optics/quantum/philosophical comparison. This is a variation, not automatically an error.
- **Approved handling:** Review the Grade 11–12 physics progression as a two-year sequence. Keep Grade 12 atomic physics if it serves local standards, but ensure no major conceptual gaps/duplication.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf Resources Grade 11 includes atomic/radiation physics; Grade 12 emphasizes optics and epistemology/quantum transition.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – Physics
- **Decision:** Keep as-is

### Lớp 12 — Modernization — REVIEW (High)
- **Location:** G4 / investment math
- **Current / observation:** Investment math emphasizes business/investment and a startup project.
- **Why flagged:** Useful modern adaptation, but financial education should include uncertainty and risk, not only opportunity.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve; add compound interest, inflation, probability/risk, diversification concepts, fees/taxes as locally relevant, fraud/scam recognition and ethical/social effects of investment.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf mathematics includes applications to economics; modern financial literacy benefits from explicit risk judgment.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – Mathematics
- **Decision:** Revise

### Lớp 12 — Modernization — REVIEW (Medium)
- **Location:** No explicit ICT/AI block
- **Current / observation:** No clear Grade 12 digital/AI ethics and systems progression is documented.
- **Why flagged:** For a contemporary curriculum, students need informed judgment about AI, data, manipulation, intellectual property and human-machine relations.
- **Approved handling:** Consider adding or integrating these themes into technology, literature/media, philosophy or thesis work rather than creating an isolated tech-only curriculum.
- **Reference/rationale:** Waldorf Resources Grade 12 ICT explicitly includes AI, virtual reality, robotics, manipulation of data, legal/economic implications.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – ICT
- **Decision:** Revise

### Lớp 12 — Biology sequence — STRENGTH (Medium)
- **Location:** G8 / embryology and genetics
- **Current / observation:** Embryology/genetics appears in the final year.
- **Why flagged:** This is not inherently out of place; Waldorf Resources includes genetics/embryology in upper-school biology and human embryology again in Grade 12 synthesis.
- **Approved handling:** Preserve, while ensuring modern genetics, ethics and reproductive-health accuracy.
- **Reference/rationale:** Upper-school Waldorf biology includes genetics and embryology.
- **Source:** Waldorf Resources – Natural Science
- **Decision:** Keep as-is
